at epic close (the close PR is the archival PR). Newest first within each
section.

- **Findings, Important** (to Story 2.1.15):
  1. **The warehouse's "one run of three" does not hold at a Reader's
     edge.**
     - **MEASURED, the studies' text:** the warehouse has 9–12 Readers
       ([20]). Each Reader's network hops on its own
       (`radio-and-location.md` l.229–231, l.484), and its Field Generators
       time their slots from its beacon (l.476, l.631–633), in a block after
       that beacon (l.544). The study orders the bays "aisle by aisle … so
       an item's set (its bay and the bay on either side) is one run of
       three" (l.656–658), and `parts-and-toolchains.md` § [27] prices every
       item tag that way (l.520–524). Neither study says how a tag hears a
       neighbouring bay whose Field Generator follows another Reader.
     - **INFERRED, the failure:** along an aisle longer than one Reader's
       bay of about 30 m, an item at the edge has a neighbour in another
       Reader's block, at that Reader's frame time and, in the US, on its
       channel. That set cannot be one run: the tag wakes at least twice,
       and holds a second network's timing and hop order.
     - **INFERRED, its cost, reproduced by the pass from § [27]'s inputs:**
       a second wake for that slot, widened for 12 s of drift as the first
       is, takes the listen from 1.51 to 2.44 µA. A CR2032 then needs
       163.8 mAh over five years, 0.92 of [27]'s five years, and the
       cold-chain tag 176.4 mAh, 0.85. Widened only for the two Readers'
       crystals (20 ppm × 12 s), it needs 138.3 mAh (1.08) and 151.0 mAh
       (0.99). The cold-chain tag at a Reader's edge is unmet on paper
       either way, and the plain tag on the first.
     - **Why Important:** § [27] says the 12 s listen keeps five years on a
       CR2032 "for both item tags", and the sitting chooses the item tag's
       cell from that margin. The study's recommendation, the CR2450
       wherever the item takes it, still meets [27] at a Reader's edge
       (about 2.0 times the need, INFERRED). So Sprint 4's D2 default stands
       and no ASK-OMID row is filed. The gap dates from Story 2.1.10's
       neighbour sets, and Story 2.1.12's sentence states the run.
- **Findings, Minor** (to Story 2.1.15):
  2. **The count's beacon wakes are unpriced for the warehouse**
     (`radio-and-location.md` l.665–672). INFERRED: "commands are rare
     beside a day's 86,400 listens" counts the 1 s listen, and the warehouse
     tag listens 7,200 times a day. Each step of the count wakes every tag
     under that Reader for the next beacon (8.77 µC). The cold-chain tag's
     14.4 mAh margin is about 5.9 million such wakes over five years, so it
     holds only while its Reader steps the count less often than about once
     in 27 s.
  3. **The frame's figures leave out the joining window and the downlink
     slot** (l.413–416 and l.531–533, beside l.420–440). MEASURED: the frame
     counts the beacon and the block only, and the conference's 327 reply
     slots fill its frame with nothing over. INFERRED: 1,308 a second and
     "up to 164" are upper bounds. [3] still holds, with about 300 slots a
     second to spare.
  4. **The VNA's box holds cables** (`tiered-cost.md` l.421–424). MEASURED
     by the pass at R&L's page (RL-NANOVNAH4), after the read of 23:47:36Z:
     its "Packing list" holds "15cm SMA male to male RG316RF cable x2" and
     "SMA female to female connector x1". "LOAD KIT (SUPPLIED)" is one of
     the page's paid accessories, beside a "TEST JIG FOR TESTING YOUR OWN
     CIRCUIT" at 9.95. The study says the page lists "no cable to a board".
     No total changes.
  5. **A tag that hears nothing widens its window without bound**
     (l.536–543, beside l.633–635). INFERRED: the window grows by 2 × 60 ppm
     of the time since the tag last heard a slot, so the 6.6 µA of a lost
     tag holds only while it hears something. A tag shut in a drawer passes
     a 0.25 s frame's window after about 35 minutes. From then on, a search
     every 12 s can listen for a whole frame, up to about 120 µA.

The standard (hub KB `sprint-proposal-standard`) was read by its steps 0–8
at their rule level, and its friction log's newest entry, P74, the one
Sprint 3's proposal read last. Neither it nor KB
`plan-authoring-for-cold-execution` changed on the hub's origin/main since
that read; the one tool that did is the sprint parser (hub #4543, a relative
plan path and a CRLF plan), whose origin/main copy this proposal ran
(§ Verified-live). The Sprint 3 review's advice to this proposal is carried:
R1 and R4 in § Independent checks, R3 and F7 in the merge-loop line, F12
and F13 in D7, and F18 in leg 3's gate.

**Why this order (step 2):**
- **Leg 1** goes first. Finding 1 is the review's first Important finding,
  and the only one whose D2 may need Omid's word before the sitting (D2), so
  it gets the most time ahead of it. Its outcome may move a sentence of
  `tiered-cost.md` § Both cells, which leg 2 re-reads.
- **Leg 2** follows. Its prices are dated as near the sitting as the sprint
  allows, and it reads leg 1's cell recommendations. It shares
  `parts-and-toolchains.md` with leg 1 (§ The debugger here; § The battery
  and § The toolchains there), so serial order spares the file a conflict.
- **Leg 3** goes last. It gates nothing in the study, and it touches
  `scripts/`, so it merges by Omid's word; placing it last keeps the
  sitting's path from waiting on that merge. It shares only the plan, the
  manifest and the friction log with legs 1 and 2.
- **Stories 2.1.7 and 2.1.8 stay outside the sprint.** The sitting is
  Omid's, and no executor seat can hold it. The close needs the sitting's
  spec and a closer who did none of the epic's work.

**The sprint's shape (step 3).** Three legs, under the cap.
- **Story 2.1.12 is split**, by the standard's session-fit test. As filed,
  it names about eight mechanisms with checks of their own: the listening
  layout; the battery re-pricing with D2; the plate's supply; the debugger's
  cable and the tags' fixture; JLCPCB's minimum; the three-board arithmetic;
  GNU make's citation; and the conference's block. That is above the test's
  six or so, and one leg's plan would come to ten or eleven tasks, above
  D9's tripwire of eight. The seam is the story's own two clauses:
  - **2.1.12 keeps the listening window,** "the tags' battery figures … on a
    listening layout": finding 1, finding 6 and the anti-collision nit, in
    two of the three studies.
  - **2.1.14 takes the tiers,** "the tiers' totals on a budget that can
    build what it lists": findings 2–5 and the other four nits, in
    `tiered-cost.md` and one pointer line of `parts-and-toolchains.md`
    § The debugger.

  Each item moves as the review wrote it, with what it MEASURED and what it
  INFERRED, and the manifest moves in lockstep.
- **Weighed and declined:**
  - **2.1.12 whole, in one leg, with D9's seam named.** Its estimate is
    above the tripwire, so the executor would stop before its first task and
    split it there. The remainder would then be a new story outside this
    sprint, and a second sprint ahead of the sitting.
  - **Story 2.1.13 in a later sprint.** It is ready and small, and it holds
    the manifest's one RED tooth besides the sitting's. A later sprint costs
    a proposal and a review for a five-task leg.
  - **Legs 1 and 2 at once, on two boxes.** They share no study section, but
    each ticks the same plan and appends its `reviewedFor` line to the same
    place in `docs/close/2.1.json`, so the second leg would rebase by hand.
    It saves one leg's wall-clock, which the sitting's date does not hang
    on.

1. **Leg 1 — Story 2.1.12** — the listening window, in
   `docs/study/radio-and-location.md` and `docs/study/parts-and-toolchains.md`:
   finding 1's layout chosen and stated (D3), its cost carried into every
   battery figure it moves, and Sprint 3's D2 re-run for [9], [18] and [27]

   ward's (the anti-collision nit); GNU make's platforms cited, or named
   among the tools not cited (finding 6, D4). Gate: the story's greps quoted

After the sprint, `rebuild-spec-approved` (Story 2.1.7) stays RED.

**Decisions (step 5) — no decision currently blocks execution on Omid.** Each
is the executor's at its default unless Omid says otherwise at the approval.

- **D1 — A study chooses; the sitting decides** (Sprints 2 and 3's D1,
  unchanged). A number Omid set or accepted in `docs/study/requirements.md`
  changes only by his word. A figure that a dated pointer line there quotes,
  and that this sprint changes, gets a new dated pointer line beside it; the
  text stays otherwise unchanged.
- **D2 — A margin that fails on paper after finding 1's layout.** If [9],
  [18], [26] or [27] is unmet on paper at the planning capacity:
  - the leg records it, names the routes with their costs (measure first, a
    larger cell, a slower listen where a latency allows it, one conversion
    per reading, the fallback in which the Field Generators listen) and
    recommends one, and the sitting decides. No requirement is lowered.
  - **A baby's band.** The nursery's room tag is a baby's tag ([16], [17]),
    and the study says a baby's band "may not" take a CR2450
    (`parts-and-toolchains.md` l.457). So if [18] fails for the room tag and
    the larger cell is the only route that meets it on paper, no route meets
    [18] in a baby's band. The leg then puts it to Omid first-hand in its
    terminal, framed by the goal [18] serves (a tag changed at a planned
    yearly service, never mid-stay: `requirements.md` [18]) with the leg's
    recommendation for that goal, and records his words with the terminal's
    two bracketing `date -u` reads. If he cannot be reached in the leg, it
    files ASK-OMID A2 with that question.
  - Default: the number stands, and the study recommends the closest route.
    The leg proceeds on the default, and its tick names A2 if it filed one.
  - INFERRED at proposal, for the leg to check and not a finding: a tag that
    hears only its set's slots, skipping the beacon, would listen for about
    8.1 µA at 1 s (2.795 µC + 5.8 mA × 0.92 ms, once a second), against the
    13.9 µA printed and the review's 16.73 µA for a second wake. If that
    layout's costs hold, the clause above may not fire.
- **D3 — Finding 1's layout is the leg's choice.** The options the review
  names, and one it implies:
  - a tag that skips the Reader's beacon on most listens, keeping time on its
    32 kHz crystal and re-syncing on its own zone's slot. Its cost: what the
    beacon carries (the downlink that names a listen set; in the US, the hop
    to the next channel) waits for the next beacon the tag hears;
  - a short sync before each group of slots, so that each zone's set follows
    a sync. Its cost is frame time for each group, which the anti-collision
    figure carries;
  - the second wake, charged: the review's 16.73 µA for the room tag;
  - another the leg finds.

  The leg weighs each on its listening cost, on [13]'s 5 s, [17]'s 5 s and
  [22]'s 5 min, on the frame time [3] needs, and on the downlink that names
  a set. It states the chosen one in `radio-and-location.md` § How a tag
  hears a Field Generator it has not heard before, and carries its cost into
  `parts-and-toolchains.md` § The battery's Listening row, § Each worn tag's
  year and § [27].
- **D4 — Finding 6: cite it or name it.** GNU make's platforms are cited
  from a page that states them, with its URL and fetch date, or the sentence
  above the table names GNU make beside the Arm GNU Toolchain and the SDK as
  not cited. GNU's own make page reset the connection twice for Story 2.1.10
  (`docs/reference-research.md` § 4); if it refuses again, the sentence is
  the default. Either way, the closer's `three-platform-claims-sourced`
  reads one true sentence.
- **D5 — Prices** (Sprint 3's D5, unchanged). In US dollars, each from a
  seller's public page, with the date it was read, the quantity it is for
  and the page's URL; cited, never pinned; shipping and tax left out, and
  the document says so. Each tier's total states what it is made of.
- **D6 — A part the studies leave unnamed** (the 100 W USB-PD supply, the
  debugger's 10-pin cable, the tags' adapter or pogo-pin fixture) is priced
  as a representative part the leg names, marked "representative, for
  pricing" with the reason. It is never presented as a choice: the build
  initiatives choose it.
- **D7 — A page that refuses a plain request** is put to Omid in ONE message
  in the leg's first minutes, listing every such URL with what each price is
  for and the leg's default if it stays unread. The research log's § 4 names
  the sellers that refused Sprint 3 and LCSC's pace (15 s between requests),
  so the list is drawn before the first price is read (the review's
  disposition of F12 and F13). His answer is recorded first-hand, with the
  terminal's two bracketing `date -u` reads. Default, if he cannot be
  reached in the leg: each is written not read and named in its tier's
  Lacks, and no total counts it as zero; that was his answer for Sprint 3's
  five ("Write all as not read (Recommended)").
- **D8 — Milestone 1 is priced for the US build** (Sprint 3's D8,
  unchanged). The parts that differ for the EU build are named, not priced.
- **D9 — A leg is a session.** The tripwire: an executor's plan above eight
  tasks stops before its first task, narrows the leg in story order, splits
  the remainder off as a new story under the same step, and logs the miss
  against this proposal. The seams:
  - **Leg 1:** finding 6 becomes the remainder. Finding 1 and the
    anti-collision nit stay, since the layout sets the block the nit
    computes.
  - **Leg 2:** finding 5 and the four nits become the remainder. Findings
    2–4 stay, since they move the totals the sitting reads.
  - **Leg 3:** none; it is small.

  A session limit mid-leg pushes the branch and leaves a notes DRAFT, and
  the resumed sitting re-runs the re-verify set first.
- **D10 — Every claim is sourced or marked** (Sprints 2 and 3's D10,
  unchanged): every number and every "the rule allows" or "the part
  supports" sentence names its source, is MEASURED with the read, or is
  INFERRED with its arithmetic. Also binding: no figure from the 2005/2017
  datasheets becomes a requirement (spec Appendix B); no medical claim (spec
  C8); no third-party text, figure or file is copied into the repo (the
  licence lane); nothing from the reference folder is committed; the Outlook
  message stays unread (reading rule 7).
- **D11 — The tool still never fetches** (Sprint 3's D11). Leg 3 changes the
  drift line, the stub and the reading rules; `docs/reference-repos.md`'s
  "The tool never fetches a file row" stands.
- **D12 — Merges.**
  - **Legs 1 and 2 are docs-only** (`docs/**`). Each merges by `gh pr merge
    --squash --match-head-commit <full sha>` on green, under the session's
    merge delegation as it stands, or by Omid's word.
  - **Leg 3 touches `scripts/`.** It merges by Omid's word, which his
    in-session delegation sentence is (this repo's `CLAUDE.md` § Git
    workflow).
  - **A relayed delegation can be refused** by the auto-mode classifier. The
    leg then holds and tells the coordinator the PR and its head sha, and
    Omid's own `!` in the leg's terminal is the pass.
- **D13 — The stub's cost** (Sprint 3's F17). The check compares each
  machine's folder README with the stub byte for byte, so once leg 3 merges,
  every other machine reads `README stale-stub` until `node
  scripts/reference-check.mjs --write-stub` runs there. Leg 3 runs it on its
  own box. The other box's README is left to the check's own prompt, which
  names that command (the story's Done-when), and the next session there runs
  it when the check prints it.

**Checks per leg:**
- the flip map, through `node <hub>/scripts/close/close-epic.mjs --epic 2.1
  --repo <F> --only <ids>`;
- `node scripts/close-lint.mjs` and `npm test` (the `test` job's commands),
  unpiped, on every leg;
- `node scripts/reference-check.mjs` on leg 3, before and after
  `--write-stub`, and on any leg that adds corpus rows.

**Gate line (every leg):** the required set is `scope` + `test` (the
`protect-main` ruleset).
- **`test` runs in full on every leg**, by the test-arming reads above.
- **`scope` also gates the tick and secrets.** It runs
  `check-tick-merge-claim.mjs` (a tick never claims its own PR's merge) and
  the secret scan.

**Merge-loop line:** each leg's PR:
1. ticks its story in place: the heading gains `— ✅ DONE (date)`, with
   `**Completed:**` (the date, `tigerfid #N`, the branch and the pre-tick
   head, never the merge) and `**Outcome:**` directly under it;
2. appends its `reviewedFor` line to `docs/close/2.1.json` in the same PR
   (lockstep);
3. runs the item-8 anchor grep, `grep -n "Story 2.1.<K>"
   docs/project-plan.md docs/close/2.1.json`, and the document-name grep,
   `grep -rn "<document>.md" docs/`, under a PR-body heading titled exactly
   "Item-8 anchor grep". Each hit's disposition (refreshed, current, or
   frozen in a DONE story) stands beside it as a bullet, never a table row,
   since the hub's `check-item8-dispositions.mjs` reads bullets only (Sprint
   3's R3); each `current` one quotes its line. A predicate grep uses the
   shortest needle a line break cannot split (Sprint 3's F7);
4. runs the C8 grep, `grep -n -i -E 'medical|vital' docs/study/<document>.md`
   (each hit a negation), and reads the diff for a hostname, an IP address, a
   machine name, a private path, or any person beyond those `readme.md`
   names;
5. carries a CLAIMS AUDIT in its body: every number and every rule or part
   claim beside its source, its measurement or its INFERRED arithmetic (D10);
6. runs `node scripts/close-lint.mjs` and `npm test` unpiped, gated by `&&`
   all the way to `git commit`;
7. rebases on the fetched `main` and merges per D12, after `node
   <hub>/scripts/fleet/watch-pr.mjs <N>` from inside the clone.

No CHANGELOG.

**Kickoff (step 6, baked):** a FRESH executor session per leg, on either box:
1. **Open:** from the hub root, `node scripts/fleet-view.mjs --start
   "tigerfid Sprint 4 leg <N> — Story 2.1.<K>" --role executor --repos
   tigerfid` (the marker goes to the hub's origin by itself). It fetches and
   fast-forwards what it may. When it prints a read-at-origin line for the
   hub, the hub's tools are read at `origin/main` and the hub is never
   pulled.
2. **Fetch this repo:** on `main`, `git -C <F> fetch origin` and `git -C <F>
   merge --ff-only origin/main`, each unpiped, its exit read.
3. **Clocks:** `date -u` before every clock written.
4. **Read, before any search:**
   - this block and the leg's story, with its checks in
     `docs/close/2.1.json`;
   - `CLAUDE.md` (this repo's), § Writing in public above all; a hub-rooted
     seat reads it by hand, since it does not load there;
   - `docs/reference-repos.md`, for the reading rules, and
     `docs/reference-research.md`, for § 4's reads and refusals;
   - the study documents the story cites, the archive's Sprint 3
     `**Reviewed:**` record and its friction log's § Reviewer pass (legs 1
     and 2);
   - the previous leg's PR body and this sprint's friction log.
5. **Re-verify first:** the set below.
6. **Branch:** `story-2.1.<K>-<slug>`, from the fetched, fast-forwarded
   `main`.

Paths and habits:
- `<hub>` is the hub root's absolute path on the executor's box, `<F>` is
  `<hub>/projects/tigerfid`, and `<SCRATCH>` is the session scratchpad
  (`mkdir -p` first; on Windows `$TMPDIR` is unset).
- Checkers run unpiped, and a census never reads through `head`, `tail` or
  `cut`. Long files are written with the Write tool. `git -C` takes the
  literal path, never a shell variable (the commit guard cannot resolve one
  and judges the session's own checkout). `npm --prefix <F> test` runs the
  repo's checks without a `cd`. `close-lint` lints the repo it is run FROM:
  `node <F>/scripts/close-lint.mjs` from the hub root lints the hub and
  prints the hub's adoption date, 2026-07-06, not this repo's 2026-10-26.
  `npm test` here is light and takes no battery lock.
- On Windows, a colon path through git takes `MSYS2_ARG_CONV_EXCL='*'`, with
  the repo's path written `C:/…`, never `/c/…`, in the same command (the
  variable stops git from reading the POSIX form); no interpreter is fed by
  stdin.
- On the Mac, the lid stays open while any fleet job runs (KB
  `mac-standby-sleep-abandons-jobs`).
- CI runs on GitHub-hosted runners only. No leg edits `.github/`, and only
  leg 3 edits `scripts/`. A stranger's PR is never checked out.

**Friction log:** `docs/friction-log-sprint4-figures-that-hold.md`. Each leg
appends its section, written for a stranger:
- its context checkpoint: the plan's task count, compactions, forced
  re-reads and lost-context defects;
- each piece of friction it met;
- each guard event with the guard's own banner line quoted whole and its
  register class id beside the word "seat", so the hub's
  `check-register-seats.mjs` can pair it. A seat note says "flagged" or
  "drew the arm", never a verdict word, when a census line precedes the
  banner in its row (§ Roles).

**Re-verify at execution** (each leg, before its first edit):
- `git -C <F> log --oneline -3 origin/main` shows the previous leg's merge;
- `git -C <F> config user.email` is Omid's GitHub noreply address;
- `node scripts/close-lint.mjs` exits 0 and `npm test` is green on `main`;
- `node scripts/reference-check.mjs` exits 0 on this box. A `MISSING file`
  under `sources/` is fetched from its row's URL or copied (Sprint 2's D8);
- the flip map's teeth read as the block says through the close runner
  before the work;
- the ASK-OMID register: any row an earlier leg filed, with its answer if
  any;
- legs 1 and 2: each anchor the leg's story cites still reads as quoted in
  § Inputs swept. Leg 2 reads its `tiered-cost.md` anchors at leg 1's merge,
  and leg 1's edits move the `parts-and-toolchains.md` line numbers.

**Independent checks for the reviewer pass** (as Sprint 3's, with its R1
and R4):
- **leg 1's arithmetic:** every re-priced margin, and each scenario's block
  of Field Generator slots in its frame, reproduces from the study's own
  inputs, by a script;
- **leg 1's layout against its sources:** PSIP p23 and p24 for the clocks,
  and its downlink and hop claims against `radio-and-location.md`'s own
  § Channels and § Zones;
- **the Chorus adopt-and-decline list** of `radio-and-location.md`,
  unchanged by leg 1 unless the leg says why;
- **leg 2's prices:** a sample re-read at their URLs, any change since the
  read date named as the date's, not as an error; and every total summed
  again by a script from its rows;
- **leg 3's tests,** run against `main`'s script before the leg: the drift
  test red, the pin green;
- **the hub's two checks:** `check-register-seats.mjs` takes `--sprint
  'tigerfid — Sprint 4'`, the register's own wording (Sprint 3's R4), and
  `check-item8-dispositions.mjs` reads the legs' bullets. A reader's brief
  names each repo file as `git show <sha>:<path>` whenever the pass edits
  the tree while its readers run (Sprint 3's R1).

- **Findings, Important** (each to Story 2.1.12, ahead of the sitting):
  1. **The listening window holds for one zone's tags, not for every tag.**
     - **MEASURED, the studies' text:** each listen is charged one start-up
       and one window, "the Reader's beacon, 1.0 ms, and 0.4 ms for each
       Field Generator slot in the tag's listen set". The slots "sit in one
       block after the Reader's beacon", ASSUMED ordered "so that a tag
       hears its set in one window".
     - **INFERRED, the failure:** one block after one beacon can place only
       one zone's set next to the beacon. Every other tag wakes twice, or
       listens across the slots between them.
     - **INFERRED, its cost, reproduced by the pass from the study's
       inputs:** with the second wake timed from the beacon just heard, the
       nursery's room tag needs 16.73 µA, not 13.93. Its year needs
       171.9 mAh, 0.87 years from 150 mAh, so [18]'s "holds by 1.8 %" does
       not hold. The reader's script finds the hall's badge ([9], 2.3 %) and
       the plain item tag ([27], 4 %) losing their margins the same way.
     - **Why Important:** Sprint 3's D2 judged these margins, and the
       sitting decides the cells from them. A design that keeps one window
       a listen may exist (a tag that skips the beacon on most listens; a
       short sync before each group of slots), but the study states none.
  2. **The advanced and pro tiers buy a hot plate they cannot power.**
     MEASURED at Adafruit's page (ADA-5903, read 2026-10-29): "Note that a
     20V 5A USB PD power supply is not included!" and "You'll need to pick
     up a 100W 'laptop' power supply with USB type C PD support". Neither
     tier prices one or names it in its Lacks. That falsifies the document's
     "Each tier holds what its route needs".
  3. **No tier connects the debugger to the boards.** MEASURED at TI's
     LP-XDS110ET page (read 2026-10-29): it ships a "LaunchPad development
     kit", a "USB-A to USB-C cable" and a "Quick start guide". No cable for
     its "10-pin XDS110 out connector" comes with it. The parts study left
     that cable "not read". No tier prices it, or the tags' adapter or
     pogo-pin fixture, and no Lacks column names either.
- **Findings, Minor:**
  4. **JLCPCB's minimum assembled quantity** is written not read, and is in
     no Lacks column. INFERRED: a minimum above one board adds a module to
     each of the Reader's and the Field Generator's orders. To
     Story 2.1.12.
  5. **Three tag boards by hand "cost about the same"** is wrong by the
     document's own quote of OSH Park ("includes three copies of your
     design"). INFERRED: three designs cost 3 × 11.63 = 34.88. To
     Story 2.1.12.
  6. **GNU make's platforms** are INFERRED in its row, under a sentence

     closer's `three-platform-claims-sourced` reads that sentence. To

  7. **The reference folder's README stub** omits `sources/` and still says

  - finding 3: `parts-and-toolchains.md` line 167 charges "a one-shot
    conversion of 15.5 ms";
  - finding 4: `radio-and-location.md` line 477, "the Field Generators it
    last heard";
  - finding 5: `parts-and-toolchains.md` lines 242 ("except the Arm GNU
    Toolchain"), 248–249 ("no price stated on the page") and 251 (CMake and
    GNU make, no platform cells); `software-stack.md` lines 473–476
    ("Against 921,600 baud", "222 %");
  - the nits: `software-stack.md` 85 ("It carries no time"), 347 and 470
    ("[3]'s 1,363"); `radio-and-location.md` 292 ("no worse by the

- **Refreshed in this PR:**
  - Epic 2.1's Ordering line, which named the fix-up without its story;
  - Story 2.1.7's Context, whose "until the Sprint 3 fix-up corrects it"
    now names Story 2.1.10.
- **Observed, the closer's (Story 2.1.8):** the close runner prints "REVIEW
  SEATING MISSING" for Sprint 2's review. That review sent its fifteen seats
  to Air Traffic Control as text; the closer censuses the register.
- This PR refreshes the plan's staleness stamp.

The standard (KB `sprint-proposal-standard`) was read by its steps 0–8 at
their rule level and the newest entries of its friction log (P60–P74), not
entry by entry, with the rows Air Traffic Control's orders named: KB
`plan-authoring-for-cold-execution` item 53, a test-arming read per leg, §
Roles' "flagged" wording and the touched-file ledger.

**Why this order (step 2):**
- **Leg 1** goes first, as the review routed it: its findings 3 and 4 can
  move a cell and the tags' listening budget, which leg 2 prices. The
  corrections land before prices sit beside them.
- **Leg 2** is the epic's path to the sitting. Its prices are dated as near
  the sitting as the sprint allows.
- **Leg 3** gates nothing in the study and edits no study file. It touches
  `scripts/`, so it merges by Omid's word rather than by auto-merge, and
  placing it last keeps the sitting's path from waiting on that merge. It
  shares only the plan, the manifest and the friction log with legs 1 and 2,
  so serial order spares those files a conflict.
- **Stories 2.1.7 and 2.1.8 stay outside the sprint.** The sitting is Omid's,
  and no executor seat can hold it. The close needs the sitting's spec for
  `rebuild-spec-approved` and `build-initiatives-filed`, and a closer who did
  none of the epic's work.

**The sprint's shape.** Three legs, under the cap. Weighed and declined:
- **Two fix-up stories** (the battery and the mechanism; the rest): one
  leg's plan fits the eight-task tripwire on the estimate in § Pre-flight,
  and D9 names the seam if it does not.
- **F11 outside the sprint, as a chore:** the review routed it as a story,
  and it is small.
- **Story 2.1.6 in a later sprint, after a review of the fix-up:** leg 2
  prices both cells wherever the studies leave the choice to the sitting
  (D5), so no fix-up outcome re-prices it. A review between them would cost
  a proposal and a review for no change a price depends on.

1. **Leg 1 — Story 2.1.10** — the Sprint 2 review's fixes in
   `docs/study/software-stack.md`, `docs/study/radio-and-location.md` and
   `docs/study/parts-and-toolchains.md`: findings 1–5 and the five nits,
   with finding 4's mechanism chosen (D3) and its listening cost carried into
   the battery figures with finding 3's (D2). Gate: the story's greps quoted
   in the PR body, each hit's disposition beside it, and a claims audit over
   every changed figure. Flip map: `fcc-ids-both-named` FAIL → PASS; the four
   study teeth stay PASS. Prose for the closer: `studies-review-fixes`, with
   `three-platform-claims-sourced`, `free-tools-three-platforms` and
   `no-medical-claim` read again where the leg edits. Item-8 scope: the
   three studies, `docs/study/requirements.md`'s dated pointer lines (D1),
   and Story 2.1.7's Context.
2. **Leg 2 — Story 2.1.6** — `docs/study/tiered-cost.md`: milestone 1 per
   device, per build route (hand-solder, partly assembled, fully assembled)
   and per tool tier (starter, advanced, pro), with D9's bench and a total
   per tier, every price dated (D5–D8). Flip map: `tiered-cost-doc` FAIL →
   PASS. Prose for the closer: `tiers-dated` and `no-medical-claim`. Item-8
   scope: the three studies' lines that hand work to 2.1.6 (each § What the
   choice leaves to later stories, and `parts-and-toolchains.md` § The
   debugger's "Story 2.1.6 prices the rest").
3. **Leg 3 — Story 2.1.11** — the reference check names the fetch route for
   a missing `sources/` file or folder (D11). Gate: the new tests seen red
   against `main`'s script, then green, with `npm test` green. Flip map: no
   tooth flips. Prose for the closer: `reference-check-sources-route`.
   Item-8 scope: `docs/reference-repos.md` and `docs/reference-corpus.tsv`'s
   header comment, where they describe the tool's messages.

After the sprint, `rebuild-spec-approved` (Story 2.1.7) stays RED.

**Decisions (step 5) — no decision currently blocks execution on Omid.** Each
is the executor's at its default unless Omid says otherwise at the approval.

- **D1 — A study chooses; the sitting decides** (Sprint 2's D1, unchanged).
  A number Omid set or accepted in `docs/study/requirements.md` changes only
  by his word. A figure that a dated pointer line there quotes, and that this
  sprint changes, gets a new dated pointer line beside it; the text stays
  otherwise unchanged.
- **D2 — A margin that fails on paper after the re-pricing.** If findings 3
  and 4 together leave [9], [18], [26] or [27] unmet on paper at the planning
  capacity:
  - the leg records it, names the routes with their costs (measure first, a
    larger cell, a slower listen where a latency allows it, the fallback in
    which the Field Generators listen) and recommends one, and the sitting
    decides. No requirement is lowered.
  - Only if no route meets the number does the leg put it to Omid first-hand
    in its terminal, framed by the goal the number serves with the
    recommendation for that goal (the review's friction R3), and record his
    words. If he cannot be reached in the leg, it files ASK-OMID A2 with that
    question. Default: the number stands, and the study's recommendation is
    the closest route. The leg proceeds on the default.
- **D3 — Finding 4's mechanism is the leg's choice.** The review's example is
  the Reader's beacon naming the slots of the zone's neighbours. Others the
  leg may weigh: the Field Generators' beacons grouped in one block after the
  Reader's beacon, or a periodic wider listen. The leg weighs each on its
  listening cost, on [13]'s 5 s move and on [22]'s 5 min, states the chosen
  one in `radio-and-location.md` § Zones, and carries its cost into
  `parts-and-toolchains.md` § Each worn tag's year and § [27].
- **D4 — Finding 5: cite it or mark it.**
  - **The tools:** each tool's platforms, install and licence are cited from
    its maker's page by URL and fetch date, or marked not read. A licence
    read from the tool's own licence text names it.
  - **The wire frame:** the line rate the table is measured against is
    sourced or marked ASSUMED. The module's 3 MBaud UART (PSIP p26) is
    stated beside it, the USB bridge's own limit stays with the build
    initiatives, and one sentence says whether the binary frame still wins
    and why.
- **D5 — Prices.**
  - In US dollars, each from a seller's public page, with the date it was
    read, the quantity it is for and the page's URL.
  - Cited, never pinned: no choice rests on a price page (Sprint 2's D8).
  - Shipping and tax are left out, and the document says so.
  - Where the studies leave a choice to the sitting (the item tag's cell; the
    nursery tag's cell if the measurement falls short), both options are
    priced.
  - Each tier's total states what it is made of: which devices, at which
    build route, with which tools and bench.
- **D6 — A part the studies leave unnamed** (the USB bridge, the regulator,
  the relays, the passives, the enclosures) is priced as a representative
  part the leg names, marked "representative, for pricing" with the reason.
  It is never presented as a choice: the build initiatives choose it.
- **D7 — A page that refuses a plain request** (as the FCC's, FTDI's and
  Microchip's did for Sprint 2: F8, F13) is put to Omid early in the leg, in
  ONE message listing every such URL with what each price is for and the
  leg's default if it stays unread. His answer is recorded first-hand, with
  the terminal's two bracketing `date -u` reads. A price no one reads is
  written "not read", and the tier's total names what it lacks. No total
  counts an unread price as zero.
- **D8 — Milestone 1 is priced for the US build** (915 MHz parts), since
  Omid, its first builder (spec D9), builds it there. The parts that differ
  for the EU build are named, not priced.
- **D9 — A leg is a session.** The tripwire: an executor's plan above eight
  tasks stops before its first task, narrows the leg in story order, splits
  the remainder off as a new story under the same step, and logs the miss
  against this proposal. The seams:
  - **Leg 1:** findings 1, 2 and 5 and the five nits become the remainder.
    Findings 3 and 4 stay, because they can move what leg 2 prices.
  - **Leg 2:** the tool tiers and D9's bench become the remainder. The
    devices and the build routes stay.
  - **Leg 3:** none; it is small.

  A session limit mid-leg pushes the branch and leaves a notes DRAFT, and the
  resumed sitting re-runs the re-verify set first.
- **D10 — Every claim is sourced or marked** (Sprint 2's D10, unchanged):
  every number and every "the rule allows" or "the part supports" sentence
  names its source, is MEASURED with the read, or is INFERRED with its
  arithmetic. Also binding:
  - no figure from the 2005/2017 datasheets becomes a requirement (spec
    Appendix B);
  - no medical claim (spec C8);
  - no third-party text, figure or file is copied into the repo (the licence
    lane);
  - nothing from the reference folder is committed;
  - the Outlook message stays unread (reading rule 7).
- **D11 — F11's fix is the message, not a fetcher.** A `--fetch` arm that
  downloads `sources/` rows is declined:
  - **its cost:** network code in the tool, and tests that need a stand-in
    for the network;
  - **its benefit:** eight rows, fetched once per machine;
  - **its risk:** sellers refuse plain requests (F13), so the tool would fail
    where a browser succeeds.

  The tool keeps its rule, "The tool never fetches a file row"
  (`docs/reference-repos.md`).
- **D12 — Merges.**
  - **Legs 1 and 2 are docs-only** (`docs/**`). Each merges by `gh pr merge
    --squash --match-head-commit <full sha>` on green, under the session's
    merge delegation as it stands, or by Omid's word.
  - **Leg 3 touches `scripts/`.** It merges by Omid's word, which his
    in-session delegation sentence is (this repo's `CLAUDE.md` § Git
    workflow).
  - **A relayed delegation can be refused** by the auto-mode classifier. The
    leg then holds and tells the coordinator the PR and its head sha, and
    Omid's own `!` in the leg's terminal is the pass.

**The touched-file ledger** (Sprint 2's F21: leg 4's grants left out the
research log). Every file each leg may edit, so the coordinator's grants name
them all. Every leg edits `docs/project-plan.md` (its tick),
`docs/close/2.1.json` (its `reviewedFor` line) and this sprint's friction log.

  `docs/ci-contract.md` does not change: `test` already runs the test file.

**Pre-flight (step 4), per leg** (KB `plan-authoring-for-cold-execution`

**Checks per leg:**
- the flip map, through `node <hub>/scripts/close/close-epic.mjs --epic 2.1
  --repo <F> --only <ids>`;
- `node scripts/close-lint.mjs` and `npm test` (the `test` job's commands),
  unpiped, on every leg;
- `node scripts/reference-check.mjs` on every leg that adds corpus rows, and
  on leg 3.

**Gate line (every leg):** the required set is `scope` + `test` (the
`protect-main` ruleset).
- **`test` runs in full on every leg**, by the test-arming reads above.
- **`scope` also gates the tick and secrets.** It runs
  `check-tick-merge-claim.mjs` (a tick never claims its own PR's merge) and
  the secret scan.

**Merge-loop line:** each leg's PR:
1. ticks its story in place: the heading gains `— ✅ DONE (date)`, with
   `**Completed:**` (the date, `tigerfid #N`, the branch and the pre-tick
   head, never the merge) and `**Outcome:**` directly under it;
2. appends its `reviewedFor` line to `docs/close/2.1.json` in the same PR
   (lockstep);
3. runs the item-8 anchor grep, `grep -n "Story 2.1.<K>"
   docs/project-plan.md docs/close/2.1.json`, and the document-name grep,
   `grep -rn "<document>.md" docs/`, under a PR-body heading titled exactly
   "Item-8 anchor grep" (the hub's `check-item8-dispositions.mjs` reads no
   other title: the review's friction R1). Each hit's disposition stands
   beside it (refreshed, current, or frozen in a DONE story), and each
   `current` one quotes its line;
4. runs the C8 grep, `grep -n -i -E 'medical|vital' docs/study/<document>.md`
   (each hit a negation), and reads the diff for a hostname, an IP address, a
   machine name, a private path, or any person beyond those `readme.md`
   names;
5. carries a CLAIMS AUDIT in its body: every number and every rule or part
   claim beside its source, its measurement or its INFERRED arithmetic (D10);
6. runs `node scripts/close-lint.mjs` and `npm test` unpiped, gated by `&&`
   all the way to `git commit`;
7. rebases on the fetched `main` and merges per D12, after `node
   <hub>/scripts/fleet/watch-pr.mjs <N> --repo tigerfid`.

No CHANGELOG.

**Kickoff (step 6, baked):** a FRESH executor session per leg, on either box:
1. **Open:** from the hub root, `node scripts/fleet-view.mjs --start
   "tigerfid Sprint 3 leg <N> — Story 2.1.<K>" --role executor --repos
   tigerfid` (the marker goes to the hub's origin by itself). It fetches and
   fast-forwards what it may. When it prints a read-at-origin line for the
   hub, the hub's tools are read at `origin/main` and the hub is never
   pulled.
2. **Fetch this repo:** on `main`, `git -C <F> fetch origin` and `git -C <F>
   merge --ff-only origin/main`, each unpiped, its exit read.
3. **Clocks:** `date -u` before every clock written.
4. **Read, before any search:**
   - this block and the leg's story, with its checks in
     `docs/close/2.1.json`;
   - `CLAUDE.md` (this repo's), § Writing in public above all; a hub-rooted
     seat reads it by hand, since it does not load there;
   - `docs/reference-repos.md`, for the reading rules, and
     `docs/reference-research.md`, for § 4's search ideas;
   - the study documents the story cites, and the archive's Sprint 2
     `**Reviewed:**` record (leg 1);
   - the previous leg's PR body and this sprint's friction log.
5. **Re-verify first:** the set below.
6. **Branch:** `story-2.1.<K>-<slug>`, from the fetched, fast-forwarded
   `main`.

Paths and habits:
- `<hub>` is the hub root's absolute path on the executor's box, `<F>` is
  `<hub>/projects/tigerfid`, and `<SCRATCH>` is the session scratchpad
  (`mkdir -p` first; on Windows `$TMPDIR` is unset).
- Checkers run unpiped, and a census never reads through `head`, `tail` or
  `cut`. Long files are written with the Write tool. `git -C` takes the
  literal path, never a shell variable (the commit guard cannot resolve one
  and judges the session's own checkout). `npm --prefix <F> test` runs the
  repo's checks without a `cd`. `close-lint` lints the repo it is run FROM:
  `node <F>/scripts/close-lint.mjs` from the hub root lints the hub and
  prints the hub's adoption date, 2026-07-06, not this repo's 2026-10-26.
  `npm test` here is light and takes no battery lock.
- On Windows, a colon path through git takes `MSYS2_ARG_CONV_EXCL='*'`, and
  no interpreter is fed by stdin.
- On the Mac, the lid stays open while any fleet job runs (KB
  `mac-standby-sleep-abandons-jobs`).
- CI runs on GitHub-hosted runners only. No leg edits `.github/`, and only
  leg 3 edits `scripts/`. A stranger's PR is never checked out.

**Friction log:** `docs/friction-log-sprint3-ready-for-the-sitting.md`. Each
leg appends its section, written for a stranger:
- its context checkpoint: the plan's task count, compactions, forced
  re-reads and lost-context defects;
- each piece of friction it met;
- each guard event with the guard's own banner line quoted whole and its
  register class id beside the word "seat", so the hub's
  `check-register-seats.mjs` can pair it (it read none of Sprint 2's, which
  were written as prose). A seat note says "flagged" or "drew the arm",
  never a verdict word, when a census line precedes the banner in its row (§
  Roles).

**Re-verify at execution** (each leg, before its first edit):
- `git -C <F> log --oneline -3 origin/main` shows the previous leg's merge;
- `git -C <F> config user.email` is Omid's GitHub noreply address;
- `node scripts/close-lint.mjs` exits 0 and `npm test` is green on `main`;
- `node scripts/reference-check.mjs` exits 0 on this box. A `MISSING file`
  under `sources/` is fetched from its row's URL or copied (Sprint 2's D8);
- the flip map's teeth read as the block says through the close runner
  before the work;
- the ASK-OMID register: any row an earlier leg filed, with its answer if
  any;
- leg 1 only: each finding's anchor above still reads as quoted.

**Independent checks for the reviewer pass** (as Sprint 2's):
- **leg 1's quotes against their sources:** the tag's `AWI_API.h` for
  `logTimestamp`, and Smart Tracker's source read as live code, not comment
  blocks (Sprint 2's F22); the eCFR's §2.803; PSIP p16 and p26; TMP117 p6
  and p12; SWRA797 p14 and p17; MDN's Web NFC data;
- **leg 1's arithmetic:** every re-priced margin reproduces from the study's
  own inputs, by a script;
- **the Chorus adopt-and-decline list** of `radio-and-location.md` and
  `software-stack.md`, unchanged by leg 1 unless the leg says why;
- **leg 2's prices:** a sample re-read at their URLs, any change since the
  read date named as the date's, not as an error;
- **leg 3's tests,** run against `main`'s script before the leg, red.

**After the sprint:** the last leg's executor stamps the block as executed:

### Sprint 2 — "The study makes its choices: a radio a beginner may legally run, the parts and free tools to build it, and host software for any laptop" (proposed 2026-10-27; 4 legs, serial) — ✅ APPROVED (2026-10-27) — ✅ EXECUTED (2026-10-28)

repo-rooted executor seat on Air Traffic Control's orders. None was elevated.

- **#39, after the stamp:** read as a record of Omid's decisions, not as a

- **Findings, Important:**
  1. **The seal's HF decision said more than the recommendation it cited.**
     - **MEASURED:** #39 recorded "no space reserved for one on its core"
       under Omid's "I like all those recommendations". The written
       recommendation, unchanged since #36, says "The HF memory stays a
       fitting option on the core, for a later tier" (`software-stack.md`),
       and `parts-and-toolchains.md` says the same.
     - **The basis it records is wrong too.** "A web page reads NFC in one
       browser only": MDN's data lists Web NFC in Chrome, Opera, Samsung
       Internet and WebView on Android. That is still Android only, so
       milestone 1's no-HF decision stands.
     - **Omid's ruling,** first-hand in this pass's terminal between its
       `date -u` reads of 10:01:36Z and 10:27:02Z, after a recommendation:
       "Reserve footprint, coil off-core (Recommended)". The core keeps an
       unfitted footprint for an NFC memory, its I²C link and two antenna pads
       at the board's edge. The coil lives in the enclosure, away from the
       module's antenna and its ground plane, which TI asks to be "as large as
       possible" with "as little as possible routing" (SWRA797 §3.1.2).
     - **Fixed in this PR:** Story 2.1.7's Context.
     - **Routed to the Sprint 3 fix-up:** `software-stack.md`'s Decided line,
       its table row and the "one browser only" sentence. The two "fitting
       option" lines are right as they stand.
     - **Open for the build, INFERRED:** whether a passive NFC coil counts as
       "any other antenna" under the module's no-co-location condition (PSIP
       p54) is unread. A passive tag sends nothing of its own, so it should
       not.
  2. **The FCC grant read had no owner, and the radio study names one of
     the two IDs.** MEASURED:
     - the read "before any part is bought" is written only in Story 2.1.4's
       Outcome and its study, in no live story;
     - `radio-and-location.md` names ZAT-1312PSIP-2 alone, with no mention
       of -3. Its own pinned source points to "fccid.io/ZAT-1312PSIP-3"
       (SWRA797 p14) and lists the -3 filings (p17).

     **Fixed in this PR:** Story 2.1.7's Context gives the read to the
     sitting, by Omid's hand in a browser, before the spec names an FCC ID.
     **Routed to the Sprint 3 fix-up:** a pointer to both IDs in the radio
     study.
  3. **Two battery margins assume the sensor's cheapest setting, where its
     cited accuracy is not specified.** MEASURED:
     - the TMP117's ±0.1 °C is specified at "8 averages, 1-Hz conversion
       cycle" (TMP117 p6), and its factory setting averages eight conversions,
       124 ms (p12);
     - `parts-and-toolchains.md` charges one 15.5 ms conversion.

     At eight averages, [18]'s year holds by 1.8 %, not 6 %. [27]'s
     cold-chain item tag at a 12 s listen holds by 1.4 %, not 5 %. The study's
     advice, to measure first and then take the larger cell, stands on a
     thinner margin. **Routed to the Sprint 3 fix-up.**
  4. **No sentence says how a tag hears a Field Generator it has not heard
     before.**
     - **MEASURED, by search across the three studies:** a tag listens only
       in "the slots of the Field Generators it last heard", and its
       listening budget is the beacon and two slots.
     - **INFERRED, the failure:** a tag carried into a room whose Field
       Generator is not among them never listens in that room's slot. Both
       the 3.0 s move and [18]'s battery assume the next listen hears it.

     **Routed to the Sprint 3 fix-up:** the mechanism, for example the
     Reader's beacon naming the slots of the zone's neighbours, with the
     listening budget re-priced.
  5. **Two choices rest on figures with no source or mark (plan D10).**
     MEASURED:
     - **The no-IDE build path:** CMake and GNU make have no platform,
       install or licence entry. "TI's free compiler" and SysConfig carry "no
       price stated on the page". The study still says every tool's platforms
       are cited except the Arm GNU Toolchain's.
     - **The binary wire frame:** the "222 %, so over the line" that decides
       it is measured against 921,600 baud, a figure with no source and no
       ASSUMED mark. The module's UART runs at 3 MBaud (PSIP p26), and the USB
       bridge's limit is unread.

     **Routed to the Sprint 3 fix-up.** The closer's
     `free-tools-three-platforms` needs the first.
  6. **Legs 2 and 3 assigned work to later stories that none of those
     stories carried** (register class
     `premark-promise-not-written-into-owning-story`; its second seat here,
     after the Sprint 1 review's finding 1). MEASURED: their studies' § What
     the choice leaves to later stories gives Stories 2.1.6 and 2.1.7 items
     those stories' text did not hold. Leg 4 wrote its own into both (#36,
     #39). **Fixed in this PR,** on Air Traffic Control's grant: one dated
     Context sentence each on Stories 2.1.6 and 2.1.7. No Done-when moved.

    tags a second; 1,363 is the radio study's slot capacity.

The standard (KB `sprint-proposal-standard`) was read by its steps 0–8 and
the newest entries of its friction log, not entry by entry.

**Why this order (step 2):**
- **Leg 1** is a short docs fix, and it goes first: three public documents
  tell strangers something no longer true, and leg 2 reads one of them.
- **Leg 2** decides what the others build on: the radio architecture and its
  legality. It answers [3] against [2] first.
- **Leg 3** names the parts for that architecture: the radio, the
  microcontrollers and their toolchains.
- **Leg 4** chooses the host software for the event schema the readers
  report, which follows leg 2's link.

**The sprint's shape.** Air Traffic Control's lean was four legs, with Story
2.1.6, the tiered cost, proposed after this sprint's review. Weighed, it
stands:
- **For:**
  - 2.1.6 prices exactly the parts leg 3 names and the radio leg 2 chooses.
    This sprint's review re-checks those choices before dated prices sit
    beside them, so a review finding that moves a part re-prices nothing.
  - A requirement put back to Omid in legs 2–3 (D2) can move a part too.
  - Prices dated nearer the sitting are fresher for it.
  - A remainder the session-fit tripwire splits off leg 2 or leg 4 (D9) has a
    sprint to land in.
- **Against:** one more proposal and review, two fresh seats, for a short
  sprint.
- **Declined:** a fifth leg for 2.1.6, gated on "no ASK-OMID row open from
  legs 2–3". That gate cannot see a review finding.

After the sprint, `tiered-cost-doc` (Story 2.1.6) and `rebuild-spec-approved`
(Story 2.1.7) stay RED.

**Decisions (step 5) — no decision currently blocks execution on Omid.** Each
is the executor's at its default unless Omid says otherwise at the approval.

- **D1 — A study chooses; the sitting decides.** Each document names its
  choice with its reasons and its rejected options, and that choice is the
  sitting's default (Story 2.1.7).
  - A number Omid set or accepted in `docs/study/requirements.md` changes
    only by his word.
  - A study that replaces an ASSUMED or INFERRED input there carries the new
    figure and its source. `requirements.md` gains a dated pointer line
    beside the old figure, and its text stays otherwise unchanged.
- **D2 — [3] against [2].** Leg 2 answers first whether one reader takes
  1,000 replies a second and still hears a worn tag 30 m away.
  - If no legal option meets [3], the leg puts the closest option, its
    figure and its cost to Omid first-hand in its terminal, and records his
    words.
  - If he cannot be reached in the leg, it files ASK-OMID A2 with that
    question. Its default: [3] stands unchanged and the study's choice is the
    closest option. The leg proceeds on the default.
- **D3 — One core or three, and the sealTag.** Leg 3 weighs §3.7's question,
  with milestone 1's sealTag as a new design inside it (the 2012 seal has no
  active radio), and names its recommendation. The sitting decides. No board
  is drawn this sprint.
- **D4 — [27], the warehouse item tag.** Leg 3 chooses between a slower wake
  and a CR2450-size cell, and states what each costs [27]'s five years. It
  goes to Omid only if neither choice keeps five years.
- **D5 — The seal's HF interface.** Leg 2 states the radio side (whether it
  needs a transmitter of its own, and the rule if so). Leg 4 states what a
  phone reading it would give the software, and records the recommendation.
  The sitting decides it with the seal's design.
- **D6 — The legality reads come before any radio is named** (spec C9). Leg 2
  reads and cites each text below, by URL and the version date the text
  prints, and names it as read before it compares radios. Where the list came
  from: spec C9 names the four US sections the tooth reads. The proposer added
  the rest from its own knowledge, not read at proposal, so the leg confirms
  each one exists and applies before citing it, and says so where one does
  not.
  - **US, 47 CFR Part 15:**
    - §15.231, periodic operation at 433 MHz;
    - §15.249 and §15.247, 902–928 MHz and 2.4 GHz;
    - §15.23, home-built devices;
    - §15.212, modular transmitters: what a pre-certified module's grant
      lets a beginner build without authorisation of their own;
    - Subpart F, if UWB is compared.

    A dated URL (the eCFR's point-in-time view, where the leg finds it)
    pins each version. The tooth `legality-rules-cited` reads the first four
    section numbers.
  - **EU:**
    - the Radio Equipment Directive, 2014/53/EU;
    - the short-range-device harmonisation, Decision 2006/771/EC in its
      current consolidated version;
    - CEPT ERC Recommendation 70-03;
    - the harmonised standards for the bands compared: ETSI EN 300 220
      (sub-GHz), EN 300 328 (2.4 GHz) and EN 302 065 (UWB, if compared).

    Each is cited at the version the leg reads, and a text found superseded
    is cited at its successor.
  - **Limits on the reads:** public pages only (reading rule 3). A standard
    behind a paywall is cited by its number and edition, with the
    regulation's own summary in its place, and marked so.
- **D7 — Serial, not parallel.** Leg 3's radio parts follow leg 2's
  architecture, and leg 4's reader abstraction follows leg 2's link. A
  parallel 2.1.4 would weigh chips for an architecture 2.1.3 might decline.
- **D8 — Sources: pinned when a choice rests on them.** A datasheet,
  application note or reference design a choice rests on is pinned in the
  reference corpus under one folder, `sources/`, by the rules of
  `docs/reference-repos.md` § Adding to the corpus: its row, its sha256, its
  URL and licence, and its research-log entry with a verdict, in the leg's PR.
  - A law text is cited by its dated URL and its own version date, not
    pinned: the version date is its pin.
  - Pages read without a choice resting on them are cited by URL and fetch
    date.
  - The other machine fetches a new row from its URL, and it counts once its
    bytes match; otherwise it is copied from the machine that pinned it.
- **D9 — A leg is a session.** The tripwire: an executor's plan above eight
  tasks stops before its first task, narrows the leg in story order, splits
  the remainder off as a new story under the same step, and logs the miss
  against this proposal. Two seams are named now, so a split is not
  improvised:
  - **Leg 2:** the Chorus radio items and the "does ActiveWave's approach
    still win" answers become the remainder. They gate nothing in leg 3.
  - **Leg 4:** the Chorus software items become the remainder. The original
    host source stays in the leg, because the stack is chosen against it.

  A session limit mid-leg pushes the branch and leaves a notes DRAFT, and the
  resumed sitting re-runs the re-verify set first.
- **D10 — Every claim is sourced or marked.** The house style of Sprint 1's
  two documents holds:
  - every number and every "the rule allows" or "the part supports" sentence
    names its source (the law's section and version date, the datasheet's
    title, revision and page), is MEASURED with the read, or is INFERRED with
    its arithmetic;
  - an install on a platform is MEASURED (the box's OS, the date and the
    version the tool prints) or cited from the vendor's documentation (URL
    and fetch date). It is never stated as run where no session ran it
    (`three-platform-claims-sourced`).
  - **Also binding:**
    - no figure from the 2005/2017 datasheets becomes a requirement (spec
      Appendix B);
    - no medical claim (spec C8);
    - no third-party text, figure or file is copied into the repo (the
      licence lane);
    - nothing from the reference folder is committed;
    - the Outlook message stays unread (reading rule 7).
- **D11 — Merges.** Every leg is docs-only (`docs/**` alone). Each merges by
  `gh pr merge --auto --squash --match-head-commit <full sha>` under the
  session's merge delegation as it stands, or by Omid's word. None is assumed.

**Checks per leg:**
- the flip map, through `node <hub>/scripts/close/close-epic.mjs --epic 2.1
  --repo <F> --only <ids>`;
- `node scripts/close-lint.mjs` and `npm test` (the `test` job's commands),
  unpiped, on every leg;
- `node scripts/reference-check.mjs` on every leg that adds corpus rows.

**Gate line (every leg):** the required set is `scope` + `test` (the
`protect-main` ruleset, read between this session's `date -u` reads of
17:22:25Z and 17:22:56Z).
- **`test` runs in full on every leg.** Each leg ticks its story in
  `docs/project-plan.md` and edits `docs/close/2.1.json`, which the docs-only
  classifier never counts as documentation, so no leg is predicted to skip
  it.
- **`scope` also gates the tick and secrets.** It runs
  `check-tick-merge-claim.mjs` (a tick never claims its own PR's merge) and
  the secret scan.

**Merge-loop line:** each leg's PR:
1. ticks its story in place: the heading gains `— ✅ DONE (date)`, with
   `**Completed:**` (the date, `tigerfid #N` and the branch, never the merge)
   and `**Outcome:**` directly under it;
2. appends its `reviewedFor` line to `docs/close/2.1.json` in the same PR
   (lockstep);
3. runs the item-8 anchor grep, `grep -n "Story 2.1.<K>"
   docs/project-plan.md docs/close/2.1.json`, and the document-name grep,
   `grep -rn "<document>.md" docs/`, naming each hit's disposition in the PR
   body (refreshed, current, or frozen in a DONE story);
4. runs the C8 grep, `grep -n -i -E 'medical|vital' docs/study/<document>.md`
   (each hit a negation), and reads the diff for a hostname, an IP address, a
   machine name, a private path, or any person beyond those `readme.md`
   names;
5. carries a CLAIMS AUDIT in its body: every number and every rule or part
   claim beside its source, its measurement or its INFERRED arithmetic (D10);
6. runs `node scripts/close-lint.mjs` and `npm test` unpiped, gated by `&&`
   all the way to `git commit`;
7. rebases on the fetched `main` and merges per D11.

No CHANGELOG.

**Kickoff (step 6, baked):** a FRESH executor session per leg, on either box:
1. **Fetch:** `cd <hub> && git pull --ff-only && cd projects/tigerfid && git
   pull --ff-only`.
2. **Open:** `node <hub>/scripts/fleet-view.mjs --start "tigerfid Sprint 2
   leg <N> — Story 2.1.<K>" --role executor --repos tigerfid` (the marker
   goes to the hub's origin by itself).
3. **Clocks:** `date -u` before every clock written.
4. **Read, before any search:**
   - this block and the leg's story, with its checks in
     `docs/close/2.1.json`;
   - `docs/reference-repos.md`, for the reading rules, and
     `docs/reference-research.md`, for § 4's search ideas;
   - the study documents the story cites;
   - `CLAUDE.md` § Writing in public;
   - the previous leg's PR body and this sprint's friction log.
5. **Re-verify first:** the set below.
6. **Branch:** `story-2.1.<K>-<slug>`, from the fetched, fast-forwarded
   `main`.

Paths and habits:
- `<hub>` is the hub root's absolute path on the executor's box, `<F>` is
  `<hub>/projects/tigerfid`, and `<SCRATCH>` is the session scratchpad
  (`mkdir -p` first).
- Checkers run unpiped; long files are written with the Write tool; `git -C`
  takes the literal path. `npm test` here is light and takes no battery lock.
- On the Mac, the lid stays open while any fleet job runs (KB
  `mac-standby-sleep-abandons-jobs`); before a long wait, `caffeinate -i -t
  <seconds>` covers the sitting, and the lid rule still holds.
- CI runs on GitHub-hosted runners only. No leg edits `.github/` or
  `scripts/`. A stranger's PR is never checked out.

**Friction log:** `docs/friction-log-sprint2-the-study-makes-its-choices.md`.
Each leg appends its section, written for a stranger:
- its context checkpoint: the plan's task count, compactions, forced
  re-reads and lost-context defects;
- each piece of friction it met.

**Re-verify at execution** (each leg, before its first edit):
- `git -C <F> log --oneline -3 origin/main` shows the previous leg's merge;
- `git -C <F> config user.email` is Omid's GitHub noreply address;
- `node scripts/close-lint.mjs` exits 0 and `npm test` is green on `main`;
- `node scripts/reference-check.mjs` exits 0 on this box. A `MISSING file`
  from a row an earlier leg pinned is fetched from its URL or copied (D8);
- the flip map's teeth read FAIL through the close runner before the work;
- the ASK-OMID register: any row an earlier leg filed, with its answer if any.

**Verified-live at proposal** (2026-10-27, the session above):
- **The close runner.** The baseline, at `831db91`, is the verdicts above.
- **`legality-rules-cited`, executed against real files.** It ran through
  the close runner (shell: Git Bash) in a scratch worktree of `831db91`, with
  three fixture documents written for it:
  - all four sections cited: PASS (exit 0);
  - §15.23 absent, the other three present: FAIL (exit 1);
  - §15.23 as the file's last token: PASS (exit 0).

  This PR adds no run line and changes none.
- **The parser.** `node <hub>/scripts/fleet-view/check-sprint-block.mjs
  <hub> <plan> 2` read this block as proposed, exit 0:
  `{"found":true,"approvedDate":null,"executed":false,"reviewed":false,"declaredLegs":4,"legs":["2.1.9","2.1.3","2.1.4","2.1.5"],"legsSource":"structured","ambiguous":false,"sweptDate":"2026-10-27","problems":[]}`.
  - It read the plan as git stores it, with LF line endings.
  - This proposal's checkout converts to CRLF (`core.autocrlf=true`). Over
    that working-tree file the parser printed `{"found":false,"problems":[]}`
    and exit 1, because its heading pattern does not match a line that ends in
    a carriage return. That is friction F4 in the log.
- **The parser, as approved:** the same command over a scratch copy with the
  approval pasted into the heading and the label line. It read the same four
  legs, `problems: []`, exit 0; the PR body quotes its line.
- **`close-lint`:** `close-lint: ok (adopted 2026-10-26)`, exit 0.

**After the sprint:** the last leg's executor stamps the block as executed:
the heading's stamp, the Executed record, and a line saying the review is
pending. It then wraps. Then, in order:
1. the Sprint 2 reviewer pass (FRESH);
2. the Sprint 3 proposal: Story 2.1.6 and any remainder D9 split off, from
   the plan as it stands after the review;
3. the decision sitting (Story 2.1.7);
4. Epic 2.1's close (Story 2.1.8, a FRESH closer).

## Initiative 2: The design study — ✅ DONE (2026-10-31)

**Completed:** 2026-10-31 — an initiative-final close: Epic 2.1, its only epic,
closed by Story 2.1.8's PR, which cut this section whole and filed the build
initiatives, 3–7, in the plan. Its Done-when is adjudicated in the close
evidence below (`initiative-2-final`). What milestone 1's acceptance starts,
the product website's work, is carried by Story 7.1.4.

**Objective:** turn spec D1–D7 into a design a builder can execute, decided
bottom-up against three acceptance scenarios: a conference where every
attendee wears a tag and the software shows each person on the floorplan; a
hospital nursery where each baby's tag carries a temperature sensor, so
staff can find each baby and read the sensor remotely; and a warehouse
inventorying 100,000+ items in minutes. The study answers spec §3: the
requirements with numbers, how the original worked, the radio and location
architecture with its legality, the parts and free toolchains, the software
stack for Windows, macOS and Linux, and the tiered cost — then Omid's
decision sitting writes the rebuild's design spec in this repo (the tigerian
shape: a research initiative with the design sitting at its end). The
anti-goals bind every answer: nothing a beginner cannot build or debug, no
paid tool or single vendor's cloud, no medical claim, no transmitter a
beginner cannot legally run.
**Milestone 1 (filed by this study's close — spec §4):** a builder with no
experience follows `guides/` and ends with one Reader, one Field Generator
and a sealTag, a compactTag and a cardTag built; the host software running
on their own Windows, macOS or Linux machine; all three tags read together
(anti-collision); a tag carried between two zones, the move showing on the
floorplan; a tamper alarm and a temperature reading arriving; and a tag
paired with a person raising an alarm when it leaves without them. Every
shopping list is tiered (D7). Omid is the first builder (D9): milestone 1 is
accepted when he has built it from the guides, and the guides are revised
from what his build taught before anyone else is pointed at them.
**What its acceptance starts (2026-10-30):** the product's website,
activewaveinc.com, waits on it to lead with the rebuilt system. Its home page
then leads with it, and its use cases become step-by-step recipes from these
guides (fleet KB `activewaveinc-new-system-trigger`). Whoever records the
acceptance here, or Omid, starts that work.
**Done when:** Epic 2.1 is archived with close evidence (initiative-final —
Epic 2.1 is its only epic), and its close has filed the build initiatives.

#### Story 2.1.1: The requirements, from the three scenarios, with numbers — ✅ DONE (2026-10-27)

**Completed:** 2026-10-27 — tigerfid #14 (branch `story-2.1.1-requirements`).
**Outcome:** `docs/study/requirements.md` carries 27 numbers. They are the five measures for each of the three scenarios, plus each scenario's size as an assumption, and each has its reasoning and Omid's answer recorded first-hand beside it.
- **The answers.** The list went out after the session terminal's `date -u` read of 11:57:23Z, and his one-message answer came before the read of 12:05:05Z. He accepted 24 numbers as recommended and set three himself:
  - [3]: 1,000 tags/s per reader;
  - [12]: 100 tags/s in the nursery;
  - [13]: a nursery move shown within 5 s.
- **His question on [3]** ("Can we do that?") is answered in the document by the arithmetic, marked INFERRED. It needs a fast link with short replies and reader-assigned slots, or several channels per reader; the original's random delay cannot do it.
- **For Stories 2.1.3–2.1.4:** every input to the arithmetic is marked ASSUMED or INFERRED, for those stories to replace with real parts' figures.
- **The ground rules hold.** No datasheet figure is copied (spec Appendix B). The nursery is framed as finding tags and reading a sensor; every "medical" or "vital" mention is a negation (C8).
- **Story 2.1.3 answers first** the hardest pairing: [3]'s 1,000 replies a second against [2]'s 30 m with the tag worn.

**Story:** As the study, I want each scenario turned into measurable
requirements, so that every design option is judged against numbers, not
impressions.
**Context:** spec §3.1. A number for each of: indoor read range; tags per
second through anti-collision (the warehouse's 100,000+ items in minutes
implies hundreds per second — INFERRED from the arithmetic; the study sets
the number); zone accuracy; alarm latency for tamper, temperature, missed
check-in and pairing; battery life. Each number is put to Omid and his
answer recorded beside it. The old datasheets' figures conflict with each
other (spec Appendix B) and are never copied as requirements. The nursery
scenario proves "find each tag and read its temperature sensor" — never a
medical device or a monitor of vital signs (spec C8).
**Done when:** `docs/study/requirements.md` carries, per scenario, a number,
its reasoning and Omid's recorded answer for each of the five measures.

#### Story 2.1.2: How the original worked — ✅ DONE (2026-10-27)

**Completed:** 2026-10-27 — tigerfid #16 (branch `story-2.1.2-original-system`).
**Outcome:** `docs/study/original-system.md` describes the 2001–2013 system from its records at the tag `activewave-2018`: the radio link, the anti-collision scheme, the zone mechanism, the alarms and I/O, and the hardware of the Reader, the Field Generator and the three tags. Each claim cites the file it was read from or is marked INFERRED, and a script found all 208 of its quoted phrases in their sources.
- **Measured:** the packets' CRC is CRC-16/X.25. It matches the records' three test vectors and their `0xF0B8` residue.
- **No record states the radio's data rate**, its modulation or any tags-per-second figure. The requirements' 250 kbit/s stays ASSUMED, and the receiver's 330 kHz IF filter suggests the original ran slower (INFERRED).
- **Three corrections to the onboarding spec** (Context and Appendix B):
  - the compactTag is Tag 102; Tag 103 is the miniTag;
  - the Reader's radio is the Transceiver daughterboard;
  - the 2012 sealTag is an ISO 15693 logging seal with no active radio and no temperature sensor.
- **For Stories 2.1.3, 2.1.4 and 2.1.7:** milestone 1's sealTag is therefore a new design. The document's last section says why: one core in a seal enclosure, per spec §3.7, is weighed by 2.1.4 and decided by 2.1.7.

**Story:** As the study, I want one written description of the 2001–2013
system, read from its own records, so that every option has a reference to
beat.
**Context:** spec §3.2 and Appendix B, read at the tag (or in the reference
folder): the protocol documents (`api/documents/Protocol20061015.doc`,
`Protocol-tg-07092008.doc`, `AW_API.pdf`, `AW_API_V39.doc`), the Reader 103
and Tag 103 schematics and BOMs, and the sealTag's STM8L firmware source —
the only firmware source in the tree (none exists for the Standard Reader
or the PIC tags). The two-way radio: the Reader or a Field Generator wakes
and commands tags at 433 MHz; tags reply at 916/927 MHz (US) or 868 MHz
(EU) with random-delay anti-collision; a zone is a Field Generator plus a
motion detector.
**Done when:** `docs/study/original-system.md` describes the radio link, the
anti-collision scheme, the zone mechanism, the alarms and I/O, and each
milestone device's hardware, citing for each claim the file at the tag it
was read from; a claim no record supports is marked INFERRED.
